ARMAVOIDmissing8›

Research dossier: ARM — Arm Holdings PLC

Researched: 2026-06-05 · Verdict: AVOID · Conviction: 0.85 · Opportunity score: 8

Business

Arm licenses semiconductor IP — CPU instruction-set architectures and physical microprocessor designs — to chip designers who pay (a) upfront license fees for access to the technology and (b) royalties on every chip shipped. ARM v9 commands materially higher royalty rates than v8, which means ASP tailwinds persist even at flat unit volumes. Revenue is split roughly 53/47 royalties/licenses in FY2026. End markets: smartphones (~45% of chips shipped), data center (fastest-growing, royalties doubled YoY in Q4 FY2026), PC (new via Qualcomm Snapdragon X, Microsoft Copilot+ PCs, and now the Nvidia RTX Spark announcement), automotive, IoT. The durability argument is that ARM architecture is deeply embedded in the silicon supply chain — switching cost is a decade of software ecosystem lock-in, not a quarter's re-training.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

No. ARM is a great business at an untenable price. The stock has run 277% YTD to ~$393, including a ~15% single-session surge on June 1 following Nvidia's Computex announcement of RTX Spark — Arm-based Windows PC AI chips designed to run local AI agents. The fresh catalyst is real: it expands ARM's TAM in PCs, where they had limited royalty exposure historically. But a real catalyst and a good entry are not the same thing. At $393, ARM trades at 85× trailing revenue and roughly 650× trailing non-GAAP EPS. The street consensus price target sits 38% BELOW today's price (~$244 implied mean target). Even assuming 20% revenue CAGR through FY2031 and aggressive margin expansion to 35-40% non-GAAP margins, the 5-year forward math does not support $393 without requiring a premium terminal multiple that demands further multiple expansion on already historically stretched valuations. The Computex catalyst is incremental, not transformative — ARM Windows momentum (Qualcomm) already existed; this is one more tailwind, not a regime change.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • FY2026 actual (full year ended March 2026): Revenue $4.92B (+23% YoY); royalty revenue $2.61B (+21%); license revenue $2.31B (+25%). Q4 FY2026 quarterly revenue $1.49B (record).
  • GAAP EPS FY2026: $0.29 (full year). Heavy SBC suppresses GAAP earnings.
  • Non-GAAP EPS FY2026: $0.60 (full year).
  • Market cap: ~$419B. P/S: ~85×. Non-GAAP P/E: ~655×.
  • 52-week position: Near/at all-time high. Stock started 2026 ~$104; now $393. Trading ~3.8× the Jan 2026 price.
  • Street mean target: ~$244 — 38% below spot. Even analysts with a bull stance can't justify this price on fundamentals.

Fair-value derivation from this run's data:

  • FY2027 revenue estimate: ~$6.0B (20% growth, management's royalty CAGR guidance)
  • Non-GAAP net margins at 35%: ~$2.1B net income
  • At 50× forward P/E (historically very generous for a semiconductor IP company): market cap ~$105B → ~$99/share
  • At 80× forward P/E (top decile premium): ~$158/share
  • Revenue-based: $6B × 40× P/S = $240B market cap → ~$225/share

The bull case on fundamentals converges around $200-250 under generous assumptions. At $393, the stock prices in a 10-year compounding story at today's multiple — i.e., no multiple compression, perfect execution, AI PC + data center royalties accelerating above management's own guidance.

Buy-below / Fair / Stretch:

  • Buy-below: $140 (discount to fair, meaningful dip from current)
  • Fair value: $210–$245 (consistent with street consensus, 38-44× FY2027 revenue)
  • Stretch (only with data-center royalties accelerating to 30%+ CAGR sustained): $290

Current price $393 is 35–60% above even the stretch level.

Post-deal / repriced check

ARM does not fit the wait-for-deal template precisely (no lab partnership pending), but the extended-with-vs-without-catalyst framework applies directly. The Computex/RTX Spark catalyst on June 1 was a real thesis-confirming fact. However, the question per the framework is: "is the new thesis worth owning at the new price?" The RTX Spark announcement means Nvidia joins Qualcomm in shipping Arm-based Windows PC AI chips. This is incrementally bullish for royalty volumes in PCs (a new/underpenetrated segment for ARM royalties). It is NOT a regime change: ARM already dominated mobile CPUs; data center was already doubling; Windows on Arm was already established. The market's reaction — a 15% single-session surge on top of a 240% YTD run — has repriced not just this catalyst but decades of future royalties. This is "extended-with-modest-catalyst" territory. The entry edge, if it ever existed at these levels, is consumed.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: v9 royalty rate step-ups plus data center + AI PC royalties create a compounding royalty engine that hasn't even started printing in earnest — the P/S will mean-revert via earnings, not price.
  • Bear: At 85× revenue and 650× non-GAAP earnings, the stock already prices in a decade of perfect execution; any revenue growth deceleration, royalty rate disappointment, RISC-V displacement, or multiple compression (rate-cut delays are live risk right now) triggers a severe re-rating — $200 is not a crash, it's regression to an already-generous fundamental anchor.
  • First-principles: A semiconductor IP company generating $4.9B of revenue does not deserve a $419B market cap unless the market believes revenue will compound 5-7× in the next 5-7 years at expanding margins — that would be among the fastest organic revenue expansions in large-cap history, and it's already priced in before it happens.

Entry levels the agent derives

  • Buy-below: $140 — meaningful discount to fundamental fair value, accounts for rate-cut timeline pushed to late 2026/2027.
  • Fair value: $210–$245 — converges with street consensus; defensible on 38-42× FY2027 revenue.
  • Stretch (catalyst-extended only): $290 — only valid if data center royalties accelerate materially above guidance in the next 1-2 quarters.

Do not buy at $393. That price requires BOTH sustained premium multiple expansion AND fundamental execution that isn't in any base-case model.

Macro fit

Kill-switches: all clear (capex accelerating, credit spreads historically tight, Nvidia B200 demand firm). The secular AI infrastructure thesis is intact — ARM benefits from it. But the macro regime is the problem for entry here: market extended at 27× trailing P/E, rate cuts pushed to late 2026/early 2027, and the high-P/S cohort faces real multiple-compression risk without rate relief. ARM at 85× P/S is the highest-duration security imaginable in this cohort. If the June 16-17 FOMC signals cuts pushed further right, the first names to reprice are exactly these.

On the opportunity-cost lens: the marginal dollar has far better options — names below fair value with near-term catalysts or dip-buying opportunities in names with real FCF rather than option value. ARM is a "great business, wrong price" situation. Opportunity score is low because the fundamental bull case lands well below current price and the macro environment actively penalizes high-duration names.

Falsification

What would flip to HOLD (90-day):

  • A drawdown to $250-275 (25-35% from current) without a fundamental thesis break — that would revisit the stretch range.

What would flip to BUY (90-day):

  • A drawdown to $140-160 AND royalty revenue acceleration confirmed (data center royalties sustained +50%+ YoY for 2 consecutive quarters) — that would re-open a genuine entry.
  • Unexpected rate-cut acceleration (June or September FOMC) combined with a return to $200-240 range.

30-day watch items:

  • June 16-17 FOMC — any hawkish shift on cut timing is the most immediate re-rating risk.
  • RTX Spark design-win announcements and PC OEM adoption timelines — if Nvidia PC chips ship faster than expected, royalty guidance upside becomes visible.

60-day watch items:

  • Qualcomm / TSMC Snapdragon roadmap for 2026-2027 PCs (ARM royalty rate confirmation for v9 AI PC chips).
  • Q1 FY2027 (June quarter) earnings — does data center royalty growth sustain +50%+ YoY or decelerate?

90-day watch items:

  • Hyperscaler Q2 earnings (Alphabet / Microsoft, late July) — capex guidance confirmation for FY2027 is the upstream input to ARM's data center royalty outlook.
  • Any RISC-V adoption news at hyperscaler or automotive tier-1 level — structural displacement risk is slow-moving but is the long-term bear case.

not a liquidity flush. The dossier's explicit discipline: *don't catch it before the

earnings-cut cycle ends.*

Macro gate: regime extended, "hold cash, buy only real dips or fresh catalysts," and a

levered/rate-sensitive IPP into a June 16-17 FOMC with a hot May CPI headline (4.2%). That

argues for patience over a 2%-early at-market starter.

Opportunity-cost: NRG is genuinely top-of-board (#4/45, one of the few below-fair-value names

in a favored lane). This is not a "pass forever" — it's a "wait for your own number."

Entry shape

Deferred-with-trigger. Start a scale-in at ≤ $118 (small starter, ~$15-20 of the $100),

add toward ≤ $108. No timing edge to justify jumping 2% early into an accelerating decline the

day before FOMC; acting on the pre-planned level is the honest answer.

What would change my mind

  • Buy now: print ≤ $118 (start) — fires the dossier's own trigger.
  • Bull accelerant: a signed data-center power agreement lands (the un-priced catalyst) —

would justify buying even slightly above trigger.

  • Abort the thesis: an FY26 guidance cut or further EPS revisions down → drop to AVOID,

cancel the trigger (converts "transient weather miss" into "structural").

Note to future self

Watching for ≤ $118 to start, and the June 16-17 FOMC tone (a "no cuts till 2027" signal

pressures the levered multiple). Mirrored via --note.

DGXX-2026-06-10-research-buy-starter›

DGXX — BUY $12 starter (research_verdict conversion)

Decision

  • Action: BUY $12.00 notional (12% of $100 book), market order
  • Entry price: ~$6.21 (June 9 close; dossier reference price)
  • Time: 2026-06-10 UTC · Signal: 2026-06-10:DGXX:research:buy (severity 4)
  • Confidence: 0.6 · Mode: shadow — recorded, no order placed

Reasoning

My own June 9 research flipped DGXX to BUY (conviction 0.55, opportunity 62 — #1/35 on the

board). The thesis is post-pullback, pre-delivery, discount-to-comps: the $1.1B/10-yr

Cerebras MSA (May 5) is signed, the stock overshot to $9.20 and gave back ~33% to $6.21 —

inside my derived buy zone (<$6.50). At ~2.1× the $275M midpoint 2027 guide vs. operational

comps at 5–15×, the discount is an execution/dilution premium, not overvaluation. Fair value

~$9.00 (+45%), stretch $13.

Macro gate: stance says extended market, buy only real dips or fresh catalysts, prefer

below-fair-value names. DGXX passes on both counts — the 33% consolidation is a real dip and

the name is well below fair value. Kill-switches all clear.

Opportunity-cost: #1 on the board by a wide margin (62 vs. next at 42). If any name gets

the marginal dollar, it's this one.

Why only $12: single customer (Cerebras), pre-profit, near-certain dilutive raise before

Phase 1 (Dec 15), never operated at this scale, and a hawkish-risk FOMC June 16–17. Per

payoff-shape sizing: asymmetric upside but concentration risk caps the size. Starter, not

conviction weight.

Entry shape

Buy now at market. Price is already inside the pre-derived buy zone — waiting for a lower

limit is the named anti-pattern (starter-now-over-buy-below). No intraday timing edge claimed.

A hot May CPI (June 11) or hawkish FOMC dip would be an add consideration, not a regret.

What would change my mind

1. Dilution: equity raise >15% of shares outstanding → re-run the per-share math; likely trim/exit.

2. Execution: any signal Phase 1 slips past Q1 2027 → thesis broken, exit.

3. Counterparty: Cerebras financial stress or MSA renegotiation → exit immediately.

4. Level: sustained trade below $3.50 = market pricing execution failure → full reassess.

Note to future self

Watch May CPI (June 11) and FOMC (June 16–17) for a macro dip into the $5.50s — potential

add zone for a second tranche. Also watch for capital-raise announcement at any time.
